Implementation details:

```
def fast_conv(a):
    n=a.size
    m=2*n-1
    fft_len=1<<(m-1).bit_length()
    fa=np.fft.rfft(a, fft_len)
    return np.fft.irfft(fa*fa, fft_len)[:m]
```
For n large (32768), m=65535, fft_len=65536, okay. For gradient cross: p length m (2n-1), a length n; we need grad_i = sum_j p_{i+j} a_j for i=0..n-1. We can compute via FFT as earlier: rev_a length n, p length 2n-1. conv length 3n-2. M=3n-2; fft_len=next_pow2(M). But we only need indices n-1 .. 2n-2? For i=0..n-1 index i+n-1 length n. Exact score:
```
def exact_score(a):
    a=np.asarray(a,float).ravel()
    b=fast_conv(a)
    S=a.sum()
    if S <= 0: return inf
    return 2*a.size*max(b)/(S*S)
```
LSE score/grad:
```
def lse_score_grad(a, beta, kappa=0.0):
  a=np.asarray(a,float)
  S=a.sum()
  if S<1e-12: return 1e20, zeros
  p=a/S
  b=fast_conv(p)
  b = np.maximum(b,0)
  bm=b.max()
  z=beta*(b-bm)
  # avoid under/overflow; z<=0
  ez=np.exp(z)
  L=bm + np.log(ez.sum())/beta
  # if sum exp zero? no
  w=ez/ez.sum()
  grad_p = 2.0 * lse_cross(p,w) # ∂(L)/∂p? Actually ∂L/∂p = 2corr
  # J = 2n L + kappa(S-1)^2
  score = 2.0*n*L + kappa*(S-1)**2
  h=2.0*n*grad_p
  # transform to a
  dot = np.dot(h, p)  # ∑ h p
  grad_a = h/S - (p/S)*dot + 2.0*kappa*(S-1)
  return score, grad_a
```

Implement `corr_ap(p,w)`:
```
def corr_ap(p, w, n=None):
    n = p.size
    M = 3*n - 2
    ff = 1 << (M-1).bit_length()
    rev = p[::-1]
    F = np.fft.rfft(rev, ff)
    G = np.fft.rfft(w, ff)
    out = np.fft.irfft(F*G, ff)
    return out[n-1:2*n-1]
```

Resampling function top-level:
```
def resample_sequence(a, m):
    a=np.asarray(a,float).ravel()
    n=a.size
    if n==0: return np.ones(m)* (1.0/m)
    if n==m: return a
    x_old=np.arange(n,dtype=float)
    # If m > n, per 1; use np.interp
    x_new=np.linspace(0.0, n-1.0, m)
    out=np.interp(x_new, x_old, a)
    out=np.maximum(out,0)
    s=out.sum()
    if s>0: out=out/s
    else: out=np.ones(m)/m
    return out
```
Linear interpolation preserves approximate sum but normalize. Could better preserve mass using edge `right=a[-1]`. Fine.

def run_lbfgs_sequence(a, beta, maxiter, maxfun):
   n=a.size
   if a.size==0: ...
   a0=np.clip(a.astype(float),0,1000)
   # normalize? objective handles but bounds don't know sum. Let S = a0.sum(); if S < 1: a0 = a0/max(S,1e-12) to sum1.
   bounds=[(0.0, 1000.0)]*n
   res = minimize(objective, a0, args=(beta,), method='L-BFGS-B', jac=True, bounds=bounds, options=dict(maxiter=maxiter, maxfun=maxfun, ftol=1e-18, gtol=1e-14, maxcor=20, maxls=50))
   out=np.clip(res.x,0,1000)
   s=out.sum()
   if s>0: out=out/s
   return out
```

Local search peak shaving:
We need define top-level functions:
```
def softmax_weights(c, gamma):
    c=np.asarray(c,float)
    cm=c.max()
    z=gamma*(c-cm)
    z=np.clip(z, -745, 0)
    ez=np.exp(z)
    s=ez.sum()
    if s<=0: w=np.zeros_like(c); w[cm.index]=1
    return ez/s
```
But gamma for exact peak can be large; use maybe `gamma = 10000` relative to c scale? Since c ~1/n=1e-4, diff maybe 1e-7, gamma=1e6? We can use LSE weights with beta=100000? But computing gradient via many spikes may be noise. Use top T active weights uniform: T= min(n, max(5, int(0.02*n)))? For target, max coefficients might be many near central; uniform over top 500 may smooth. Or use softmax with beta=20000? We can define `active_weights(c, beta)`: same as LSE weights but for c (not LSE). The gradient direction. We can iterate:

```
def multiplicative_peak_shave(a, iters, deadline, base_beta=20000, eta0=0.1, improve_factor=0.9):
    a=np.asarray(a).copy(); S=a.sum(); p=a/S
    best=p.copy(); best_s=exact_score(p)
    for it in range(iters):
       c=fast_conv(p)
       w=soft_plus(c, base_beta)  # maybe high
       corr=corr_ap(p,w)
       h=4*n*corr # direction derivative score for p
       # multiplicative: p_new = p * exp(-eta * (h - mean?))? To keep sum fixed.
       # Entropic step: p_new ∝ p * exp(-eta*(h - hmean))
       h_centered = h - np.dot(h,p)  # ensure derivative negative
       # scale eta
       # bound eta by max negative log? Use eta = eta0 / (max(abs(h_centered))+1e-12)
       scale = max(1e-12, np.max(np.abs(h_centered)))
       eta = eta0 / scale
       logfac = -eta*h_centered
       logfac -= np.max(logfac)
       fac=np.exp(logfac)
       p_new = p*fac
       p_new /= p_new.sum()
       s_new=exact_score(p_new)
       if s_new < best_s - 1e-14:
           best=p_new; best_s=s_new
       else:
           eta *= 0.5
           # retry few times? for simplicity break inner
    return best_s, best
```
This line search can be expensive: each iteration multiple exact_score (FFT). We can evaluate `s_new` exact. Use while loop with max few retries.

The multiplicative step with h_centered might choose p_new close to p if eta small. Need tune eta0. Could test derivative: For one step, score decrease approx -eta^2 ∑p h_c^2 /? If h_c magnitude ~O(1), eta0=1 maybe. Use exponential with centered h, step can be large. We can cap logfac difference: `logfac = np.clip(logfac, -10, 10)`. Use eta0=0.5.

Maybe use standard gradient line search instead:
```
dir = -h_centered
p_dir = np.maximum(p + eta*dir, 0)
p_dir /= sum
score
```

Pseudo:
```
def mirror_polish(p, deadline, max_updates=5000, beta_schedule=None, eta0=0.5):
    n=p.size
    p=p.copy()
    best=p.copy()
    b_s=exact_score(p)
    betas = beta_schedule or [5000,10000,20000,40000,80000]
    no_improve=0
    for beta in betas:
       for _ in range(max_updates):
          if time.monotonic()>deadline: break
          c=fast_conv(p)
          c=np.maximum(c,0)
          cm=c.max()
          # weights
          z=beta*(c-cm)
          ez=np.exp(np.clip(z,-700,0))
          s=ez.sum()
          if s>0: w=ez/s
          else: w=np.zeros_like(c); w[np.argmax(c)]=1
          corr=corr_ap(p,w)
          h=4.0*n*corr
          hc=h - float(np.dot(h,p))
          hn=np.linalg.norm(hc, ord=np.inf)
          if hn < 1e-14: break
          eta=eta0/hn
          # Armijo multiplicative
          improved=False
          for attempt in range(6):
             logf=-eta*hc
             logf=logf - logf.max()
             logf=np.clip(logf,-15,15)
             fac=np.exp(logf)
             p_new=p*fac
             sn=p_new.sum()
             if sn<=0: eta*=0.5; continue
             p_new/=sn
             sn_score=exact_score(p_new)
             if sn_score < b_s - 1e-15:
                 p=p_new; b_s=sn_score; improved=True; if b_s<best_s update
                 break
             else:
                 eta*=0.5
          if improved:
             no_improve=0
             if b_s<best_s: best=p.copy(); best_s=b_s
          else:
             no_improve+=1
             if no_improve>20: break
       if time... break
    return best_s,best
```
Computing exact_score twice per accepted (corr weights + score) costly. We can compute c currently and score current from c instead of exact_score. After p_new, compute exact. We can avoid computing `b_s` via exact_score initially? We have c from loop. Use current score from c. Need update when p changes.

Optimization: In loop, after c and weights, we know `current_score = 2n*cm/(p sum=1)^2` = 2n*cm. Use that. Then p_new score exact. This saves one exact per iteration. If p_new fails, current score remains. If p_new accepted, we could use its exact score and c maybe not; next loop recompute.

`exact_score` on normalized p returns 2n*max(fast_conv(p)). Good.

def directional_polish(p, deadline, max_rounds=2000, beta=20000):
   current=sfrom c
   best...
   for it:

      corr=corr_ap(p,w); h=4n*corr; h-=dot

      neg = d<0
      if any neg: eta_max = min(p[neg]/(-d[neg])) else inf

Implement `lse_weights` efficiently:
```
def lse_weights(b, beta):
    bm = float(b.max()) if b.size else 0.0
    z = beta * (b - bm)
    # z <=0 maybe roundoff; clip
    z = np.minimum(z, 0.0)
    z = np.maximum(z, -700.0)
    ez = np.exp(z)
    s = ez.sum()
    if s <= 0 or not np.isfinite(s):
        w = np.zeros_like(b)
        w[int(np.argmax(b))] = 1.0
        return w
    return ez/s
```
If beta<=0, uniform.

`lse_score_grad`:
```
def lse_score_grad(a, beta, kappa):
    a = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
    n = a.size
    if n == 0:
       return 1e30, np.zeros(0)
    S = float(a.sum())
    if S <= 1e-15:
       return 1e30, np.ones(n)
    p = a / S
    c = fast_conv(p)
    c = np.where(c > 0.0, c, 0.0)
    cm = float(c.max())
    if beta <= 1.0:
       # maybe uniform? still compute LSE? For beta small, use average? Use beta=1.
       z = c - cm
       ez = np.exp(z) # z<=0; cm max
    else:
       z = beta*(c-cm)
       z = np.minimum(z, 0.0)
       z = np.maximum(z, -700.0)
       ez = np.exp(z)
    s = ez.sum()
    if s <= 0: ... # uniform? But max coeff one -> L=cm, w max
    L = cm + np.log(s)/beta
    score = 2.0*n*L + kappa*(S-1.0)*(S-1.0)
    if s == 1: w zeros.
    w = ez/s
    corr = corr_ap(p,w)
    h = 4.0*n*corr
    dot = float(np.dot(h,p))
    grad = (h - dot) / S + 2.0*kappa*(S-1.0)
    # ensure finite
    if not np.all(np.isfinite(gradient)):
       grad = (h-dot)/S
    return score, grad
```
Potential if beta small (e.g. 10): z may underflow to 0 for all except max? Actually b differences can be up to 1e-4, beta=10 diff -0.001, many visible. Good.

`run_lbfgs`:
```
def run_lbfgs(a, beta, maxiter, maxfun, kappa=1e-6):
    a = np.clip(np.asarray(a,float), 0.0, 10.0).copy()
    S=a.sum()
    if S<1e-12 or a.size<2:
       return a
    a = a / S  # start sum 1
    n=a.size
    bounds = [(0.0, 10.0)] * n
    try:
       res = minimize(lse_objective, a, args=(beta, kappa), method='L-BFGS-B', jac=True, bounds=bounds, options={'maxiter': maxiter, 'maxfun': maxfun, 'ftol': 1e-18, 'gtol': 1e-14, 'maxcor': 20, 'maxls': 50})
    except Exception:
       return normalize_sequence(a)
    x = np.clip(np.asarray(res.x,float),0.0,10.0)
    x = normalize_sequence(x)
    return x
```
`lse_objective` top-level:
```
def lse_objective(a, beta, kappa): return lse_score_grad(a,beta,kappa)
```
`normalize_sequence` returns copy sum 1 (or uniform if small). Values maybe >10? if n small edge sqrt maybe >10? n small; cap? For n=100, a0~0.1; no. For n=1, clip. We can clip to [0,1000].

`mirror_polish` details. Need use `exact_score_norm` maybe faster: It normalizes input if not. Use p normalized. Score from c:
```
def exact_score_normal(p):
   c=fast_conv(p); c=np.maximum(c,0); S=p.sum(); if S<1e-12: return inf; return 2*p.size*float(c.max())/(S*S)
```
Since p sum 1, `2*n*cm`.

Mirror polish should update best when current exact improves. We can compute current at start. But inside loop we recompute c and use cm; if p normalized, current=2n*cm. Use that.

Pseudo with top-level:
```
def mirror_polish(p, deadline, max_updates_total=3000, eta0=0.5, betas=None):
    p = normalize_sequence(np.asarray(p,float))
    n = p.size
    if n < 2:
       return exact_score(p), p
    best = p.copy(); best_s = exact_score(p)
    if betas is None:
       betas = [2000, 5000, 10000, 20000, 50000, 100000]
    updates_budget = max(100, max_updates_total)
    per_beta = max(10, updates_budget // len(betas))
    for beta in betas:
       no_improve=0
       for _ in range(per_beta):
          if time.monotonic() > deadline: break
          c = fast_conv(p); c=np.maximum(c,0)
          cm = float(c.max())
          current = 2.0 * n * cm  # since sum=1
          if current < best_s and np.isfinite(current):
             best_s=current; best=p.copy()
          if not np.isfinite(current): break
          w = lse_weights(c,beta)
          corr = corr_ap(p,w)
          h = 4.0 * n * corr
          dot = float(np.dot(h,p))
          hc = h - dot
          infn = float(np.max(np.abs(hc)))
          if infn < 1e-15: break
          eta = eta0 / infn
          improved=False
          for _ in range(6):
              logf = -eta * hc
              mf = float(np.max(logf))
              logf = logf - mf
              logf = np.clip(logf, -20.0, 20.0)
              fac = np.exp(logf)
              pn = p * fac
              sn = float(pn.sum())
              if sn <= 0 or not np.isfinite(sn):
                   eta *=0.5; continue
              pn /= sn
              sn_score = exact_score_normal(pn)  # does conv again
              if sn_score < current - 1e-15:
                   p=pn; current=sn_score; improved=True
                   if current < best_s: best_s=current; best=p.copy()
                   break
              else:
                   eta *=0.5
          if improved:
             no_improve=0
          else:
             no_improve +=1
             if no_improve > 30: break
       if time.monotonic() > deadline: break
    # final exact
    final_s=exact_score_normal(best)
    if final_s < best_s: best_s=final_s
    return best_s,best
```
This computes `exact_score_normal(pn)` which redoes FFT. We also have current c and could compute `pn` score maybe with `fast_conv` in exact. Can't avoid. But okay.

Potential problem: Multiplicative update with `hc` can drastically change distribution if eta too large; line search fixes. If no improvement with high beta, beta might be too focused; try lower? We schedule increasing. Could in each stage if no improvement start next beta; okay.

Maybe use top active weights uniform to get more exact subgradient when beta too high. We can define `topk_weights(c, k)`. In mirror_polish, for each beta, after some iterations, if no improve, switch to top uniform. Simpler: Use weights `w = lse_weights(c, beta)` but if beta high, uniform over top if sum exp small? `lse_weights` with exp may put only max; multiplicative one peak. That could be okay for exact max but may miss secondary peaks; later low beta handles. Alternative combine: `w = 0.5*lse_weights + 0.5*uniform_top`? Hard.

Let's add a separate `topk_polish` function. For exact max, top T weights uniform. It might be more effective at final. Implement using same mirror steps with weights top T, beta ignored.
```
def topk_weights(c, ncoef):
   k = min(len(c), max(1, ncoef))
   idx = np.argpartition(c, -k)[-k:]
   w=np.zeros_like(c); w[idx]=1/k; return w
```
Then mirror loop can pass weight function parameter? Top-level `mirror_polish` with `weight_mode` option. To avoid many functions, include in mirror_polish a `use_topk` loop.
Maybe simpler: In `mirror_polish`, for each beta, after beta stage, if no improve, do a few topk stages with k in [5,20,100,500]. But code lengthy. We can also just run mirror_polish with beta schedule; the LSE weights approximate topk as beta→∞. Good.

Another local method: "active coefficient clipping" using direct polynomial operations. Suppose active coefficient k. To reduce b_k, we can solve for a small scaling of a segments: For k, b_k = [a:0..min(k,n-1)] dot reversed segment. We can reduce entries that are large. We already have gradient.

Let's think about interpolation and optimization order. If we run L-BFGS on 32768 raw parent, the parent has been optimized at 6962 but not for 32768; L-BFGS must refine finite-size details. - Quick resample parent to 4096? We can run a short L-BFGS at 8192 or 16384 (less expensive) to get good shape.
- Then resample to 32768 and polish.

2. Resample mid result to `main_len` = next power (16384 or 32768) and run L-BFGS high β.
3. Mirror polish both; pick best.
This hierarchy can exploit lower dimension.

       per = remaining / float(max(1,total-i))
       maxfun = int(per / eval_est)
       if maxfun < 50: maxfun=50
       if maxfun > max_fun_cap: maxfun=max_fun_cap
       maxiter = min(1500, max(50, maxfun//2))
       a=run_lbfgs(a,beta,maxiter,maxfun)
       s=exact_score(a)
       if s < best_s: best_s=s; best_a=a.copy()
    return best_s,best_a

LP might be the differentiator. Let's design a top-level `lp_direction_update(p, margin_scale=1.0, max_n=1024)`:
```
def lp_direction_update(p, max_n=1024, time_budget=60):
    n=p.size
    if n > max_n or n < 8: return p, exact_score(p)
    p=normalize_sequence(p)
    c=fast_conv(p); cm=float(c.max()); if not finite or cm<=0: return p,float('inf')
    M=cm*(1+1e-9)
    # Build A_ub
    js=np.arange(n)
    ii=np.arange(n)
    rows=(js[:,None]+ii[None,:]).ravel()
    cols=np.repeat(js,n)
    data=np.tile(p,n)
    try:
       mat=sp.csr_matrix((data,(rows,cols)), shape=(2*n-1,n))
    except Exception:
       return p, exact_score(p)
    c_obj=-np.ones(n)
    b_ub=np.full(2*n-1, M)
    bounds=[(0.0,None)]*n
    t0=time.monotonic()
    try:
       res=linprog(c_obj, A_ub=mat, b_ub=b_ub, bounds=bounds, method='highs', options={'time_limit': min(120, max(10, time_budget))})
    except TypeError:
       # maybe no time_limit
       res=linprog(...)
    if not res.success or res.x is None: return p, exact_score(p)
    g=np.clip(res.x,0,None)
    Sg=g.sum()
    if Sg <= 1e-9: return p, exact_score(p)
    g=g/Sg
    # Try blends
    best=p; best_s=exact_score(p)
    for alpha in [1.0,0.8,0.6,0.4,0.2,0.0]:
       q=alpha*p + (1-alpha)*g
       q=normalize_sequence(q)
       qs=exact_score(q)
       if qs < best_s: best_s=qs; best=q
    return best,best_s
```
Since p itself is feasible? Yes. LP optimum sum may be 1 with different g; blends may or may not help. If alpha=1 is p; alpha=0 g. Maybe small blending with p? Use fractions t of g: q=(1-t)p+t g with t in [0,1], including 1. If LP optimum g has sum>1, q with t=1 normalized g might violate its own constraints; but blended maybe. Try t=0.1..1.

Detailed function implementations:

```
import time
import numpy as np
from scipy.optimize import minimize
import operator

def normalize_sequence(a):
    a = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64).ravel().copy()
    if a.size == 0:
        return np.ones(1)
    a = np.clip(a, 0.0, 1000.0)
    s = float(a.sum())
    if s <= 1e-15 or not np.isfinite(s):
        return np.ones(a.size, dtype=np.float64) / a.size
    return a / s
```
Return sum 1. Values maybe >1000 if n small? clipped before normalization could make sum not 1? If clip reduces, re-normalize. Fine.

`is_safe_vector`? no.

`fast_conv`:
```
def fast_conv(a):
    a=np.asarray(a,dtype=np.float64).ravel()
    n=a.size
    if n == 0: return np.zeros(0)
    m=2*n-1
    fft_len=1 << (m-1).bit_length()
    fa=np.fft.rfft(a, fft_len)
    c=np.fft.irfft(fa*fa, fft_len)[:m]
    # remove tiny negative
    # c = np.where(c > 0, c, 0)
    return c
```
If n=1, m=1, bit_length(0)? `(m-1).bit_length()` =0, 1<<0=1, rfft length1 okay.

`corr_ap`:
```
def corr_ap(p, w):
    n=p.size
    if n==0: return np.zeros(0)
    m=3*n-2
    if m <=0: return np.zeros(n)
    fft_len=1 << (m-1).bit_length()
    fp=np.fft.rfft(p[::-1], fft_len)
    fw=np.fft.rfft(w, fft_len)
    conv=np.fft.irfft(fp*fw, fft_len)
    return conv[n-1:2*n-1]
```
For n=1, m=1, out[0:1] okay.

`exact_score`:
```
def exact_score(a):
    a=np.asarray(a,float).ravel()
    n=a.size
    if n==0: return np.inf
    S=float(a.sum())
    if S <= 1e-15 or not np.isfinite(S): return np.inf
    c=fast_conv(a)
    c=np.maximum(c,0.0)
    mx=float(c.max()) if c.size else 0.0
    val=2.0*n*mx/(S*S)
    if not np.isfinite(val): return np.inf
    return float(val)
```
Use `float(c.max())`. Good.

Exact score normal uses same; no need separate.

`lse_weights` as above.

`lse_score_grad`: Need handle if `beta` very large. Use `np.logaddexp`? We use exp sum. For many zeros, sum exp maybe 1; fine. If beta small, z = beta*(c-cm); c-cm maybe 0 to -1e-4, beta 10 -> -0.001; no underflow. If beta=1e6 and n large, z min -1000; clip -700. Good.
```
def lse_score_grad(a,beta,kappa=1e-6):
   a=np.asarray(a,float).ravel()
   n=a.size
   if n==0: return 1e30, np.zeros(0)
   S=float(a.sum())
   if S<=1e-15: return 1e30, np.ones(n)
   p=a/S
   c=fast_conv(p)
   # c nonneg
   c=np.where(c>0.0, c, 0.0)
   cm=float(c.max()) if c.size else 0.0
   if cm <= 0:
      return 2.0*n*cm + kappa*(S-1)**2, np.zeros(n) + ...? gradient? If cm=0 (a zero except?), p normalized conv not zero. For uniform? no.
   if beta <= 0:
      w = np.full_like(c, 1.0/c.size)
      L = float(c.mean()) # but LSE with beta -> max? For beta=0, log-sum-exp ->? Let's just not use beta 0. Schedule min 10.
   else:
      z=beta*(c-cm)
      z=np.minimum(z,0.0)
      z=np.maximum(z,-700.0)
      ez=np.exp(z)
      s=ez.sum()
      if s<=0 or not np.isfinite(s):
         w=np.zeros_like(c); w[int(np.argmax(c))]=1.0; L=cm
      else:
         w=ez/s
         L=cm + np.log(s)/beta
   score=2.0*n*L + kappa*(S-1)*(S-1)
   corr=corr_ap(p,w)
   h=4.0*n*corr
   dot=float(np.dot(h,p))
   grad=(h-dot)/S + 2.0*kappa*(S-1.0)
   # clean nonfinite
   if not np.all(np.isfinite(grad)):
      grad=np.zeros(n)
   return score, grad
```
If beta large and s= number of max; log(s)/beta small. Good.

`lse_objective` returns this. Need no closures.

`run_lbfgs`: If `maxfun`<2? set. For n large, `bounds` list creation. Use `a0` clip 0,10. If S<1e-12, uniform.
```
def run_lbfgs(a,beta,maxiter,maxfun,kappa=1e-6):
    a=np.clip(np.asarray(a,float),0,10); a=normalize_sequence(a)
    n=a.size
    if n<2: return a
    bounds=[(0.0,10.0)]*n
    opt={'maxiter': int(maxiter), 'maxfun': int(maxfun), 'ftol':1e-18, 'gtol':1e-14, 'maxcor':10, 'maxls':40}
    try:
       res=minimize(lse_objective,a,args=(beta,kappa),method='L-BFGS-B',jac=True,bounds=bounds,options=opt)
    except Exception:
       return a
    x=np.clip(np.asarray(res.x,float),0,10)
    return normalize_sequence(x)
```
If `maxiter` 0, L-BFGS? set at least 1.

`optimize_lbfgs_schedule`:
```
def optimize_lbfgs_schedule(a,betas,deadline,eval_est=None,max_fun_cap=3000):
    a=normalize_sequence(a); n=a.size
    best_s=exact_score(a); best_a=a.copy()
    if eval_est is None:
       t=time.monotonic(); lse_score_grad(a,betas[0] if betas else 50,1e-6); eval_est=max(1e-4, time.monotonic()-t)
    # maybe scale cap by n: if n>20000 cap=2000
    if n>30000: cap=max(500,min(max_fun_cap,2000)) else cap=max_fun_cap
    total=len(betas); i=0
    for beta in betas:
       i+=1
       if time.monotonic() >= deadline: break
       rem=deadline-time.monotonic()
       per=rem/float(max(1,total-i+1))
       maxfun=int(per/eval_est)
       if maxfun<80: maxfun=80
       if maxfun>cap: maxfun=cap
       maxiter=min(1200, max(50, maxfun//3))
       a=run_lbfgs(a,beta,maxiter,maxfun)
       s=exact_score(a)
       if s<best_s and np.isfinite(s): best_s=s; best_a=a.copy()
       # optional update eval_est? skip
    return best_s,best_a
```

```
def mirror_polish(p, deadline, max_updates=None, betas=None, eta0=0.5):
    p=normalize_sequence(np.asarray(p,float)); n=p.size
    if n<2: return exact_score(p), p
    if max_updates is None:
       if n<=4096: max_updates=3000
       elif n<=16384: max_updates=1800
       else: max_updates=900
    betas = betas or [500,2000,5000,10000,20000,50000]
    best=p.copy(); best_s=exact_score(p)
    per=max(20, max_updates//len(betas))
    no_stage=0
    for beta in betas:
       no_improve=0
       for _ in range(per):
          if time.monotonic()>=deadline: break
          c=fast_conv(p); c=np.maximum(c,0.0); cm=float(c.max()); current=2.0*n*cm
          if current < best_s: best_s=current; best=p.copy()
          if not np.isfinite(current): break
          w=lse_weights(c,beta)
          corr=corr_ap(p,w)
          h=4.0*n*corr
          dot=float(np.dot(h,p)); hc=h-dot
          scale=float(np.max(np.abs(hc)))
          if scale < 1e-15: break
          eta=eta0/scale
          improved=False
          for _ in range(5):
             logf=-eta*hc
             mx=float(np.max(logf)); logf=logf-mx; logf=np.clip(logf,-18,18)
             fac=np.exp(logf)
             pn=p*fac; sn=float(pn.sum())
             if sn<=0 or not np.isfinite(sn): eta*=0.5; continue
             pn/=sn
             sn_s=exact_score(pn)
             if sn_s < current - 1e-15:
                p=pn; current=sn_s; improved=True
                if current<best_s: best_s=current; best=p.copy()
                break
             else:
                eta*=0.5
          if improved:
             no_improve=0
          else:
             no_improve+=1
             if no_improve > min(60, per//2+10): break
       if time.monotonic()>=deadline: break
       if no_improve > ...: pass
    final_s=exact_score(best)
    if final_s < best_s: best_s=final_s
    return best_s,best
```

```
def resample_nearest(a,m):
    a=np.asarray(a,float).ravel(); n=a.size
    if n==1: return full m a[0]
    if n==m: return a.copy()
    if m <= 0: return np.ones(1)
    # map new index to old with half-shift
    x = (m-1)/(n-1) * np.arange(m) if n>1 else zeros
    idx = np.floor(x + 0.5).astype(np.int64)
    idx=np.clip(idx,0,n-1)
    out=a[idx]
    return normalize_sequence(out)
```

```python
"""
Multi-stage search for non-negative sequences minimizing
    F(a) = 2n * max(conv(a,a)) / (sum(a))^2

Main ideas:
  - Work with normalized probability vectors to remove scale invariance.
  - Use an LSE-smoothed maximum-convolution objective and L-BFGS-B.
  - Start from the provided parent, resample it to several lengths,
    optimize mid-size, then upscale the optimized shape to larger lengths.
  - Finish with an entropic / multiplicative active-coefficient polishing step.
  - All helper functions are top-level, no closures or lambdas.
"""

import time
import numpy as np
from scipy.optimize import minimize

# Global used by scipy callback to enforce per-L-BFGS-run time limits.
_OPT_DEADLINE = 0.0


def _lbfgs_callback(ck):
    if time.monotonic() > _OPT_DEADLINE:
        raise RuntimeError("local time limit exceeded")


def normalize_sequence(a):
    """Return a non-negative finite normalized copy. Sum is 1 if possible."""
    a = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
    if a.size == 0:
        return np.ones(1, dtype=np.float64)
    a = np.nan_to_num(a, nan=0.0, posinf=1000.0, neginf=0.0)
    a = np.clip(a, 0.0, 1000.0)
    s = float(a.sum())
    if s <= 1e-15 or not np.isfinite(s):
        return np.ones(a.size, dtype=np.float64) / float(a.size)
    return a / s


def fast_conv(a):
    """Linear convolution a*a by real FFT."""
    a = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
    n = a.size
    if n == 0:
        return np.zeros(0, dtype=np.float64)
    m = 2 * n - 1
    fft_len = 1 << (m - 1).bit_length()
    fa = np.fft.rfft(a, fft_len)
    c = np.fft.irfft(fa * fa, fft_len)[:m]
    return c


def corr_ap(p, w):
    """
    For p length n and w length 2n-1, compute
        r_i = sum_{k=i}^{i+n-1} p_{k-i} * w_k
    for i=0..n-1.  This is the gradient kernel for convolution P^2.
    """
    p = np.asarray(p, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
    w = np.asarray(w, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
    n = p.size
    if n == 0:
        return np.zeros(0, dtype=np.float64)
    m = 3 * n - 2
    fft_len = 1 << (m - 1).bit_length()
    fp = np.fft.rfft(p[::-1], fft_len)
    fw = np.fft.rfft(w, fft_len)
    out = np.fft.irfft(fp * fw, fft_len)
    return out[n - 1:2 * n - 1]


def exact_score(a):
    """Exact evaluation of the sequence score."""
    a = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
    n = a.size
    if n == 0:
        return np.inf
    s = float(a.sum())
    if s <= 1e-15 or not np.isfinite(s):
        return np.inf
    c = fast_conv(a)
    c = np.maximum(c, 0.0)
    mx = float(c.max()) if c.size else 0.0
    val = 2.0 * float(n) * mx / (s * s)
    if not np.isfinite(val):
        return np.inf
    return float(val)


def exact_score_normalized(p):
    """Exact score for p assumed to have sum 1."""
    p = np.asarray(p, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
    n = p.size
    if n == 0:
        return np.inf
    c = fast_conv(p)
    c = np.maximum(c, 0.0)
    mx = float(c.max()) if c.size else 0.0
    val = 2.0 * float(n) * mx
    if not np.isfinite(val):
        return np.inf
    return float(val)


def lse_weights(c, beta):
    """Weights proportional to exp(beta*(c - max(c)))."""
    c = np.asarray(c, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
    if c.size == 0:
        return c
    cm = float(c.max())
    if beta <= 0.0:
        return np.full(c.shape, 1.0 / float(c.size), dtype=np.float64)
    z = beta * (c - cm)
    z = np.minimum(z, 0.0)
    z = np.maximum(z, -700.0)
    ez = np.exp(z)
    s = float(ez.sum())
    if s <= 0.0 or not np.isfinite(s):
        w = np.zeros(c.shape, dtype=np.float64)
        w[int(np.argmax(c))] = 1.0
        return w
    return ez / s


def lse_score_grad(a, beta, kappa):
    """
    LSE-smoothed objective for normalized p=a/sum(a), plus a tiny
    penalty on sum(a)-1 to stabilize the scale direction of L-BFGS.
    """
    a = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
    n = a.size
    if n == 0:
        return 1e30, np.zeros(0, dtype=np.float64)
    s = float(a.sum())
    if s <= 1e-15 or not np.isfinite(s):
        return 1e30, np.ones(n, dtype=np.float64)

    p = a / s
    c = fast_conv(p)
    c = np.maximum(c, 0.0)
    cm = float(c.max()) if c.size else 0.0

    if beta <= 0.0:
        w = np.full(c.shape, 1.0 / float(c.size), dtype=np.float64)
        L = float(c.mean()) if c.size else 0.0
    else:
        z = beta * (c - cm)
        z = np.minimum(z, 0.0)
        z = np.maximum(z, -700.0)
        ez = np.exp(z)
        esum = float(ez.sum())
        if esum <= 0.0 or not np.isfinite(esum):
            w = np.zeros(c.shape, dtype=np.float64)
            w[int(np.argmax(c))] = 1.0
            L = cm
        else:
            w = ez / esum
            L = cm + np.log(esum) / beta

    score = 2.0 * float(n) * L + kappa * (s - 1.0) * (s - 1.0)

    corr = corr_ap(p, w)
    h = 4.0 * float(n) * corr
    dot = float(np.dot(h, p))
    grad = (h - dot) / s + 2.0 * kappa * (s - 1.0)

    if not np.all(np.isfinite(grad)):
        grad = np.zeros(n, dtype=np.float64)
    return float(score), grad


def lse_objective(a, beta, kappa):
    return lse_score_grad(a, beta, kappa)


def run_lbfgs(a, beta, maxiter, maxfun, kappa=1e-6, deadline=None):
    """Run one L-BFGS-B step with normalized LSE objective."""
    a = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
    a = np.nan_to_num(a, nan=0.0, posinf=10.0, neginf=0.0)
    a = np.clip(a, 0.0, 10.0)
    a = normalize_sequence(a)
    n = a.size
    if n < 2:
        return a

    now = time.monotonic()
    if deadline is None:
        deadline = now + 1e9
    if deadline <= now + 0.75:
        return a

    maxiter = max(1, int(maxiter))
    maxfun = max(2, int(maxfun))

    bounds = [(0.0, 10.0)] * n
    opts = {
        "maxiter": maxiter,
        "maxfun": maxfun,
        "ftol": 1e-16,
        "gtol": 1e-13,
        "maxcor": 10,
        "maxls": 40,
    }

    global _OPT_DEADLINE
    _OPT_DEADLINE = max(now, deadline)

    try:
        res = minimize(
            lse_objective,
            a,
            args=(beta, kappa),
            method="L-BFGS-B",
            jac=True,
            bounds=bounds,
            options=opts,
            callback=_lbfgs_callback,
        )
        out = np.asarray(res.x, dtype=np.float64)
    except Exception:
        out = a

    out = np.nan_to_num(out, nan=0.0, posinf=10.0, neginf=0.0)
    out = np.clip(out, 0.0, 10.0)
    return normalize_sequence(out)


def optimize_lbfgs_schedule(a, betas, deadline, eval_est=None, max_fun_cap=3000):
    """
    Run a continuation L-BFGS schedule until deadline.
    Returns best (score, array) seen inside this schedule.
    """
    a = normalize_sequence(a)
    n = a.size
    best_s = exact_score(a)
    best_a = a.copy()

    if eval_est is None:
        t = time.monotonic()
        lse_score_grad(a, float(betas[0]) if betas else 50.0, 1e-6)
        eval_est = max(1e-4, time.monotonic() - t)
    # Be conservative: L-BFGS line search can cost more than one objective call.
    eval_est *= 1.35

    if n > 30000:
        cap = min(max_fun_cap, 2200)
    elif n > 16000:
        cap = min(max_fun_cap, 2800)
    else:
        cap = max_fun_cap

    total = len(betas)
    idx = 0
    for beta in betas:
        idx += 1
        now = time.monotonic()
        if now >= deadline:
            break

        remaining = deadline - now
        if remaining < 12.0:
            break

        per = remaining / float(max(1, total - idx + 1))
        per_deadline = now + max(6.0, 0.97 * per)

        maxfun = int(per / eval_est)
        maxfun = max(60, min(cap, maxfun))
        maxiter = min(1500, max(40, maxfun // 3))

        a = run_lbfgs(
            a,
            float(beta),
            maxiter,
            maxfun,
            kappa=1e-6,
            deadline=per_deadline,
        )
        s = exact_score(a)
        if np.isfinite(s) and s < best_s:
            best_s = float(s)
            best_a = a.copy()

    return float(best_s), best_a


def scale_betas(base_betas, n, cap=500000):
    """Scale beta schedule with sequence length."""
    scale = max(1.0, float(n) / 8192.0)
    out = []
    for b in base_betas:
        v = int(min(float(cap), float(b) * scale))
        if v < 5:
            v = 5
        out.append(v)
    return out


def resample_linear(a, m):
    """Linear resampling to length m, then normalize."""
    a = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
    n = a.size
    if m <= 0:
        return normalize_sequence(a)
    if n == 1:
        return normalize_sequence(np.full(m, float(a[0]), dtype=np.float64))
    if n == m:
        return normalize_sequence(a)
    x_old = np.arange(n, dtype=np.float64)
    x_new = np.linspace(0.0, float(n - 1), m, dtype=np.float64)
    out = np.interp(x_new, x_old, a)
    out = np.clip(out, 0.0, 1000.0)
    return normalize_sequence(out)


def resample_nearest(a, m):
    """Nearest-neighbor resampling to length m, then normalize."""
    a = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
    n = a.size
    if m <= 0:
        return normalize_sequence(a)
    if n == 1:
        return normalize_sequence(np.full(m, float(a[0]), dtype=np.float64))
    if n == m:
        return normalize_sequence(a)
    x = np.linspace(0.0, float(n - 1), m, dtype=np.float64)
    idx = np.rint(x).astype(np.int64)
    idx = np.clip(idx, 0, n - 1)
    out = a[idx]
    return normalize_sequence(out)


def default_mirror_updates(n):
    if n <= 4096:
        return 2600
    if n <= 8192:
        return 1600
    if n <= 16384:
        return 900
    if n <= 32768:
        return 520
    return 320


def mirror_polish(p, deadline, max_updates=None, betas=None, eta0=0.5):
    """
    Entropic multiplicative polishing step.  Works directly on the exact
    score after each proposed update.
    """
    p = normalize_sequence(p)
    n = p.size
    if n < 2:
        return exact_score(p), p

    if max_updates is None:
        max_updates = default_mirror_updates(n)
    if betas is None:
        betas = scale_betas([200, 600, 1500, 4000, 10000, 25000], n, cap=1000000)

    best_s = exact_score_normalized(p)
    best_p = p.copy()
    per_stage = max(30, max_updates // max(1, len(betas)))

    for beta in betas:
        no_improve = 0
        max_fail = max(12, min(80, per_stage // 3))
        for _ in range(per_stage):
            now = time.monotonic()
            if now >= deadline:
                break

            c = fast_conv(p)
            c = np.maximum(c, 0.0)
            cm = float(c.max()) if c.size else 0.0
            current = 2.0 * float(n) * cm
            if not np.isfinite(current):
                break

            if current < best_s:
                best_s = float(current)
                best_p = p.copy()

            w = lse_weights(c, float(beta))
            corr = corr_ap(p, w)
            h = 4.0 * float(n) * corr
            dot = float(np.dot(h, p))
            hc = h - dot
            scale = float(np.max(np.abs(hc)))
            if scale < 1e-16:
                break

            eta = eta0 / scale
            improved = False
            for _try in range(5):
                logf = -eta * hc
                mx = float(np.max(logf))
                logf = logf - mx
                logf = np.clip(logf, -20.0, 20.0)
                fac = np.exp(logf)
                pn = p * fac
                sn = float(pn.sum())
                if sn <= 1e-12 or not np.isfinite(sn):
                    eta *= 0.5
                    continue
                pn = pn / sn
                sn_score = exact_score_normalized(pn)
                if sn_score < current - 1e-15:
                    p = pn
                    current = float(sn_score)
                    improved = True
                    if current < best_s:
                        best_s = float(current)
                        best_p = p.copy()
                    break
                eta *= 0.5

            if improved:
                no_improve = 0
            else:
                no_improve += 1
                if no_improve >= max_fail:
                    break

        if time.monotonic() >= deadline:
            break

    final_s = exact_score_normalized(best_p)
    if np.isfinite(final_s) and final_s < best_s:
        best_s = float(final_s)
    return float(best_s), best_p


def update_best(score, a, best_score, best_a):
    if a is None:
        return best_score, best_a
    if not np.isfinite(score):
        return best_score, best_a
    if score < best_score:
        return float(score), normalize_sequence(a)
    return best_score, best_a


def record_candidate(top, score, a, cap=12):
    if a is None or not np.isfinite(score):
        return top
    a = normalize_sequence(a)
    top.append((float(score), a.copy()))
    if len(top) > cap:
        worst = 0
        for i in range(1, len(top)):
            if top[i][0] > top[worst][0]:
                worst = i
        top.pop(worst)
    return top


def to_output_list(a):
    a = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
    a = np.nan_to_num(a, nan=0.0, posinf=1000.0, neginf=0.0)
    a = np.clip(a, 0.0, 1000.0)
    s = float(a.sum())
    if s < 0.01 or not np.isfinite(s):
        return [0.5] * 64
    # Keep sum at 1 for good inactive stability and compliance.
    out = a / s
    return [float(x) for x in out]


def propose_candidate(seed=42, budget_s=2400, **kwargs):
    """
    Search for a low-score non-negative step-function height sequence.
    Returns the best list found before the time budget.
    """
    t0 = time.monotonic()
    budget = max(120.0, float(budget_s))
    safe = min(90.0, max(30.0, 0.05 * budget))
    t_end = t0 + budget - safe

    # ---------------------------------------------------------------
    # Load parent construction if available.
    # ---------------------------------------------------------------
    parent = None
    try:
        raw = height_sequence_1
        p = np.asarray(raw, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
        if p.size >= 32:
            p = np.nan_to_num(p, nan=0.0, posinf=1000.0, neginf=0.0)
            p = np.clip(p, 0.0, 1000.0)
            if float(p.sum()) > 1e-12:
                parent = normalize_sequence(p)
    except Exception:
        parent = None

    if parent is None:
        # Reasonable smooth non-negative fallback.
        n = 8192
        x = np.arange(n, dtype=np.float64) / float(n)
        p = (
            0.55
            + 0.30 * np.cos(2.0 * np.pi * x)
            + 0.18 * np.cos(4.0 * np.pi * x + 0.7)
            + 0.08 * np.sin(8.0 * np.pi * x)
        )
        p = np.clip(p, 0.01, 10.0)
        parent = normalize_sequence(p)

    pl = parent.size

    # Choose length ladder.
    if pl < 6000:
        mid_len = 4096
        main_len = 8192
        long_len = 16384
    elif pl < 14000:
        mid_len = 8192
        main_len = 16384
        long_len = 32768
    else:
        mid_len = 16384
        main_len = 32768
        long_len = 32768

    usable = max(1.0, t_end - t0)

    # Phase deadlines.
    quick_end = t0 + min(0.05 * usable, 120.0)
    mid_opt_end = t0 + 0.28 * usable
    main_opt_end = t0 + 0.58 * usable
    long_opt_end = t0 + 0.84 * usable

    best_score = np.inf
    best_a = parent.copy()
    top = []

    # ---------------------------------------------------------------
    # Quick raw candidates: parent, reversed, resampled shapes.
    # ---------------------------------------------------------------
    def add_candidate_from_array(arr):
        nonlocal best_score, best_a
        a = normalize_sequence(arr)
        s = exact_score(a)
        top = record_candidate(global_top_holder[0], s, a, cap=12)
        # Python cannot assign to nonlocal top without nested closure, so we
        # will not use this nested helper.  Deliberately unused.
        global_top_holder[0] = top
        best_score, best_a = update_best(s, a, best_score, best_a)
        return s, a

    # We cannot define closures per rules, so instead do updates manually.
    top = record_candidate(top, exact_score(parent), parent, cap=12)
    best_score, best_a = update_best(best_score, parent, best_score, best_a)

    rev_parent = parent[::-1].copy()
    s = exact_score(rev_parent)
    top = record_candidate(top, s, rev_parent, cap=12)
    best_score, best_a = update_best(s, rev_parent, best_score, best_a)

    quick_lengths = []
    for L in (mid_len, main_len, long_len):
        if L not in quick_lengths and 256 <= L <= 32768:
            quick_lengths.append(int(L))

    # Also include parent length if close to powers of two / moderate.
    if 1024 <= pl <= 32768 and pl not in quick_lengths:
        quick_lengths.append(int(pl))

    for L in quick_lengths:
        if time.monotonic() >= quick_end:
            break
        for mode in (0, 1):
            if time.monotonic() >= quick_end:
                break
            if mode == 0:
                a = resample_linear(parent, L)
            else:
                a = resample_nearest(parent, L)
            s = exact_score(a)
            top = record_candidate(top, s, a, cap=12)
            best_score, best_a = update_best(s, a, best_score, best_a)

            arev = a[::-1].copy()
            srev = exact_score(arev)
            top = record_candidate(top, srev, arev, cap=12)
            best_score, best_a = update_best(srev, arev, best_score, best_a)

    # Choose raw starts for each length.
    raw_start = {}
    for L in (mid_len, main_len, long_len):
        best_s = np.inf
        best_arr = None
        for s, a in top:
            if a.size == L and s < best_s:
                best_s = float(s)
                best_arr = a
        if best_arr is None:
            best_arr = resample_linear(parent, L)
            best_s = exact_score(best_arr)
        raw_start[L] = (float(best_s), best_arr)

    # ---------------------------------------------------------------
    # Phase 1: mid-length continuation optimization.
    # ---------------------------------------------------------------
    mid_start = raw_start[mid_len][1].copy()
    mid_betas = scale_betas([8, 20, 50, 120, 250, 500, 1000, 2000], mid_len, cap=400000)
    mid_best_s, mid_best_a = optimize_lbfgs_schedule(
        mid_start, mid_betas, mid_opt_end, eval_est=None, max_fun_cap=2600
    )
    top = record_candidate(top, mid_best_s, mid_best_a, cap=12)
    best_score, best_a = update_best(mid_best_s, mid_best_a, best_score, best_a)

    # Short mid polish.
    if time.monotonic() < main_opt_end - 30.0:
        polish_end_mid = min(main_opt_end - 20.0, t0 + 0.33 * usable)
        s, a = mirror_polish(
            mid_best_a,
            polish_end_mid,
            max_updates=default_mirror_updates(mid_len),
        )
        top = record_candidate(top, s, a, cap=12)
        best_score, best_a = update_best(s, a, best_score, best_a)
        # Keep the polished mid shape even if it did not become global best.
        if np.isfinite(s):
            mid_best_s, mid_best_a = float(s), a.copy()

    # ---------------------------------------------------------------
    # Phase 2: main length optimization.
    # ---------------------------------------------------------------
    main_start_candidates = [
        resample_linear(mid_best_a, main_len),
        resample_nearest(mid_best_a, main_len),
        raw_start[main_len][1].copy(),
    ]
    main_start = main_start_candidates[0]
    best_start_score = exact_score(main_start)
    for cand in main_start_candidates[1:]:
        cs = exact_score(cand)
        if cs < best_start_score:
            best_start_score = cs
            main_start = cand

    main_betas = scale_betas([50, 120, 250, 500, 1000, 2000, 4000, 8000], main_len, cap=800000)
    main_best_s, main_best_a = optimize_lbfgs_schedule(
        main_start, main_betas, main_opt_end, eval_est=None, max_fun_cap=3200
    )
    top = record_candidate(top, main_best_s, main_best_a, cap=12)
    best_score, best_a = update_best(main_best_s, main_best_a, best_score, best_a)

    # Main polish.
    if time.monotonic() < long_opt_end - 30.0:
        polish_end_main = min(long_opt_end - 20.0, t0 + 0.64 * usable)
        s, a = mirror_polish(
            main_best_a,
            polish_end_main,
            max_updates=default_mirror_updates(main_len),
        )
        top = record_candidate(top, s, a, cap=12)
        best_score, best_a = update_best(s, a, best_score, best_a)
        if np.isfinite(s):
            main_best_s, main_best_a = float(s), a.copy()

    # ---------------------------------------------------------------
    # Phase 3: long length optimization, if different.
    # ---------------------------------------------------------------
    if long_len > main_len:
        long_start_candidates = [
            resample_linear(main_best_a, long_len),
            resample_nearest(main_best_a, long_len),
            resample_linear(best_a, long_len),
            raw_start[long_len][1].copy(),
        ]
        long_start = long_start_candidates[0]
        best_start_score = exact_score(long_start)
        for cand in long_start_candidates[1:]:
            cs = exact_score(cand)
            if cs < best_start_score:
                best_start_score = cs
                long_start = cand

        long_betas = scale_betas([80, 200, 500, 1000, 2000, 4000, 8000], long_len, cap=1200000)
        long_best_s, long_best_a = optimize_lbfgs_schedule(
            long_start, long_betas, long_opt_end, eval_est=None, max_fun_cap=2600
        )
        top = record_candidate(top, long_best_s, long_best_a, cap=12)
        best_score, best_a = update_best(long_best_s, long_best_a, best_score, best_a)

        # Long polish.
        if time.monotonic() < t_end - 60.0:
            s, a = mirror_polish(
                long_best_a,
                t_end - 60.0,
                max_updates=default_mirror_updates(long_len),
            )
            top = record_candidate(top, s, a, cap=12)
            best_score, best_a = update_best(s, a, best_score, best_a)

    # ---------------------------------------------------------------
    # Final global polish on the best sequence found so far.
    # ---------------------------------------------------------------
    if time.monotonic() < t_end - 60.0:
        s, a = mirror_polish(
            best_a,
            t_end - 60.0,
            max_updates=default_mirror_updates(best_a.size),
        )
        top = record_candidate(top, s, a, cap=12)
        best_score, best_a = update_best(s, a, best_score, best_a)

    # If any candidate in top is better than current best, use it.
    for s, a in top:
        if np.isfinite(s) and s < best_score:
            best_score = float(s)
            best_a = a.copy()

    # ---------------------------------------------------------------
    # Choose final output.  Prefer best score, but avoid excessively
    # large lengths if remaining verification time is very small.
    # ---------------------------------------------------------------
    remaining = t_end - time.monotonic()
    max_output_len = 32768
    if remaining < 45.0:
        max_output_len = 16384
    elif remaining < 25.0:
        max_output_len = 8192

    final_a = best_a
    final_s = best_score
    for s, a in top:
        if np.isfinite(s) and s < final_s and a.size <= max_output_len:
            final_s = float(s)
            final_a = a.copy()

    if final_a.size > max_output_len:
        final_a = resample_linear(best_a, max_output_len)
        top = record_candidate(top, exact_score(final_a), final_a, cap=12)

    out = to_output_list(final_a)
    return out
```
